## A · Arithmetic Asian accuracy

RMSE measures observed deviation from a stochastic QuantLib reference, not exact truth. The shared reference SE is not divided by the number of native repeats. Estimator-only interval coverage compares against that stochastic reference while omitting reference error; it is not ordinary exact-target CI coverage. Combined native/reference uncertainty coverage is the primary uncertainty comparison. Coverage has Wilson intervals; group bootstrap intervals with three pilot groups are exploratory. A one-fixing control can have tiny residual SE and does not imply infinite efficiency.

## B · Joint portfolio price and risk

Signed leg quantities retain the full cross-leg covariance for price, delta and vega SEs. Vega is per unit absolute volatility. Hedge and scenario P&L are first-order GBM sensitivities; they are not VaR. Common-union singleton and batch comparisons use the same workload; own-grid comparisons have different workloads.

## C · Reuse at equal observed accuracy

A crossing requires the upper 95% bootstrap RMSE to meet the frozen target, budget reselection within the bootstrap, and a timing difference interval favouring reuse. Qualification is for pooled RMSE over a horizon, not a per-call accuracy guarantee. The first observed crossing is exploratory and has no familywise 95% claim across horizons or cases. No observed crossing is a valid outcome. Quadrature is a measured warm deterministic baseline; its refinement diagnostic is not a stochastic SE.
